────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

.\QDEMO\QUARDEMO.TXT
──────────────────────────────

Welcome to the Quarantine Playable Demo

Change to the directory into which the demo was installed, then
run the install program by typing 
INSTALL  [enter]
The install program will help you set up your 
keyboard/Thrustmaster steering wheel and sound device.

To run the game type
QDEMO    [enter]

Sound: 
At this time only  DMA 1, 220 address, is supported, however the IRQ can be
changed.

Quick Keyboard Reference:

Steering           - Right and left arrow keys
Accelerate         - Up arrow key
Brake / Reverse    - Down arrow key
Spacebar           - Hood Guns
M                  - Map
Esc                - Exit to Main Menu
ALT                - Select Weapon
CNTRL              - Fire Weapon
ENTER              - Horn
J                  - Jump
N                  - Nitro Boost (Argon Cells)
Q                  - Normal resolution
W                  - Low resolution(Faster screen updates)
F1                 - Look Left
F2                 - Look Ahead
F3                 - Look Right
F10                - "Curse key"

How to play:
The object of Quarantine is to escape Kemo, a prison city.  
Kemo contains five separate areas surrounded by impenetrable walls.  

Kemo's areas include :

Downtown Core- A shopping mall, highway, glass, steel and concrete. *

The Park - An open air park with canals, zoo of horrors, and scenic canyon
just to name a few highlights.

Old Kemo - Old markets, Classy residential areas, commercial districts 
and hospitals surround these tight and twisty roads.

Projects - Burning housing projects, a football stadium, and a power plant 
are a few attractions in this shell-shocked warzone.

The Wharf - Perpetually raining, the wharf contains a harbor, rail yard and 
possible escape...

*****************************************************
* Only the Downtown Core is available in this demo. *
*****************************************************

The Object:
You are Drake Edgewater, a cab driver driving a hovercab armed only with 
headlight-mounted machine-guns.  
To escape from Quarantine you must arm your vehicle with weapons, 
discover secret entrances and travel in the underground from area to area.  
To earn money,  you can pick up and deliver passengers.


!****  NOTE: In this demo all missions and plot advancement has been removed!
You can only pickup and drop off passengers and purchase weapons. In the full
game you will learn the complete story, and take on special missions to 
advance in the game.


How to Play:

Picking up passengers.

To pick up a passenger, drive around wherever you like.  
If you see a passenger waving at you, stop next to him or her.  
You will be shown a map of the Downtown Core.  The yellow arrow represents 
your current location in the city and a line indicates where to passenger 
would like to go. The estimated time of travel will be indicated in the 
lower left corner.  You will only get paid if you can deliver the passenger 
ON TIME!  You can accept or reject the fare by typing 'Y' or 'N'

Delivering a passenger.
In the cab view, a wayfinder in the lower right of the dashboard will 
indicate the distance and direction of your destination.  When the arrow is 
pointing up, you are headed in the right direction.  A compass above your 
windshield will also help you navigate.  You'll still have to do some of your 
own navigating, so if you get lost check the map by typing 'M'. A meter on the 
left side of the dashboard displays how much time is left and how much money 
you have in total.
When you reach the passenger's destination the wayfinder arrow will spin and 
the distance meter will display zero kilometers.  To drop the passenger off, 
you must stop completely on the exact destination.  The passenger will 
proceed to leave the cab and pay your fee.  If you were late, the passenger 
will pay you a reduced amount or possibly nothing, so try to be prompt!  

Navigating the Downtown Core:
Typing 'M' will bring up a map of Kemo.  If you are without a passenger you 
can set your wayfinder to any of a number of service and weapon bays.  Move 
the cursor to your desired destination with the 'arrow keys' then hit the 
spacebar.  The wayfinder will now be set to the service or weapon bay of your 
choice.  The wayfinder arrow works the same way it does when you have a 
passeng\er.  Kemo security has set up perimeter defenses around the outside 
walls of each area.  It is not advisable to use them as regular routes.

Driving:
You will be driving a modified 1952 Checker equipped with 'hovercoils'.  
Under the roads are superconductive cables providing the 'lift' which all of 
Kemo's vehicles require.  For travel off the 'power grid', batteries may be 
purchased.  Downtown Core has so few areas without a powergrid that batteries 
will not be needed in this demo.  Use the arrow keys to steer, accelerate and 
brake.
You can look out the side windows using the 'F1' or 'F3' keys and 'F2' to 
look straight ahead.

Arming yourself:
Kemo (being a prison city affected by a mind altering corporate drug 
experiment) is prone to a lot of violence.  Someone may get in your way so 
you must be equipped to deal with it.  The game starts with hood guns and 
250 bullets. Of course you probably crave MORE firepower!  In the weapon bays 
you can find a variety of weapons and ammo to suit your cab driving needs.  
Use the arrow keys and spacebar to navigate the menus.  All weapons come 
fully loaded with the maximum capacity of ammo.  If you buy another weapon of 
the same class, (Roof mounted, right fender, missile launcher or mine rack) 
you will be refunded for the existing weapon.

Combat:

You can fire your hood guns by using the spacebar (top button on a 
Thrustmaster Steering Wheel).  The bullet count is right above your steering 
wheel.
The display on the top of the screen displays the other weapons (if you have 
purchased some).  You can switch between weapons using the 'ALT' key.  The 
remaining ammo is also displayed.  To fire the selected weapon use the 'CNTRL' 
key (lower button on the Thrustmaster Steering Wheel).

Gates:

You may notice steel gates blocking the road from time to time.  The gates 
are controlled by Kemo Security.  The only way to get through them is by 
taking a passenger through whose destination is on the inside of the area.

W key:

If you have a slower machine we have incorporated a low res mode. Pressing
the 'W' key will activate this mode enabling much higher screen refreshes.
Press 'Q' to return to normal resolution.


Requirements:

386/33, 486/33 recomended
MSDOS
4 megs of RAM
640K Conventional Memory
Sound Blaster (optional)
Thrustmaster steering wheel (optional)
Joystick not implemented for DEMO!

-If there are problems running this demo on a 4 meg machine, try to disable 
smartdrv to allow for the required 4 megs. Also disabling any memory managers 
would be helpfull. Another option is to create a minimum boot disk.


The Story:
2022 Kemo City is the jewel of the West and the hovercar capital of the world.  
A prosperous city on the east coast of the United States.
Like any metropolis, Kemo City has a growing crime problem.  The inner ghetto 
area of Kemo is perceived to be a money pit where any law enforcement would be 
both futile and frivolous.  The solution is to make the old "inner Kemo" a 
demilitarized zone.

2026 Kemo City's crime problem reaches epidemic proportions.  The economy in 
the "inner Kemo" slows to a near halt.  Crime and black market trading are 
the only sources of income for inner Kemo's impoverished inhabitants.  The 
new markets and commercial districts form a ring around  "inner Kemo", but 
citizens are still stalked by the cesspool of criminal activity in the deadly 
ghetto.

2029 Ray Larabie is elected as mayor of New Kemo.  One of Ray's campaign 
promises is to build a wall around inner Kemo and eliminate the crime 
problem; the project is simply codenamed "Q".  The wall will contain a park, 
market, housing project, office core, and industrial coastal area.

2030  A ring of properties are purchased by the government around inner Kemo 
at very reasonable prices.  An armed citizen's task force assembles in 
opposition to project "Q".  Unmanned and well armored construction vehicles 
begin construction of a  5 meter thick, 10 meter high wall composed of 
plasticized concrete and steel.  Inhabitants of inner Kemo are told that the 
wall is merely a sound baffle and gates will allow passage to New Kemo.  
Within Kemo, the city is being sectioned off into five sections: Downtown 
Core, Old Kemo, Kemo Park, the Projects and the Wharf.

2031  The wall around inner Kemo nears completion.  A few of inner Kemo's 
inhabitants leave, but most return after finding no work opportunities in New 
Kemo.  Only one route leads in and out of the city, and a military presence 
is established at the lone exit.

2032  After an armed standoff,  Kemo is completely sealed off.  The only 
possible exit is the coastline, which is sealed off by an armada of 
battleships and gunboats.  Any material entering of leaving Kemo must go 
through Kemo Security.  Wanderers and criminals outside project "Q" are 
hunted down and returned by airship to their rightful place.  Outside walls 
are strewn with mines and turrets. The prison city of Quarantine is 
officially opened

2035  After three years of tribal gang wars, a citizen's committee is 
established to restore support systems such as electric power, proper food 
distribution, water filtration and activation of the old street power grids 
which support the thousands of unused hover vehicles which lay useless in the 
streets.  The gang kingpins take notice and openly support and protect the 
committee to have some of the comforts they are otherwise unable to acquire 
(including driving cool cars!).

2046  The quarantined Kemo city is doing fairly well, but unfortunately due 
to the influx of new inmates, overcrowding is becoming a problem.  New Kemo's 
OmniCorp raises public interest in the neurodrug "Hydergine 344" reported to 
eliminate "criminal thoughts" in patients.

2047  Hydergine 344 is introduced to Quarantine's water supply.  The water 
supply has an abnormally high bacterial and viral content which reacts with 
Hydergine 344 forming a pychoreactive virus.  The virus causes synaptic 
breakdown, the main symptoms being violent psychopathic tendencies.  About 
half of the prisoners have become crazed violent killer lunatics intent on 
killing anyone who is not a crazed violent killer lunatic.  

2048  The virus is spreading.



HINT:  Buy the forward guns with a free UZI then look out of your side 
view while driving. 


The final version has loads of areas super detailed mission breifings and 
loads more enemies & pedestrians and well.... the other 90% of the game!

It will be out by September so start saving your Earth dollars. 

Enjoy

# Qwebgpu

Open [plague.local:8443](https://plague.local:8443/) to play on the LAN. The Arch service reloads open tabs after edits. [Service setup](../README.md#running-qwebgpu).

For a separate loopback-only session, stop the service first or choose a different port.

```sh
python3 -m http.server 8000 --bind 127.0.0.1 --directory Qwebgpu
```

For the loopback command above, open `http://127.0.0.1:8000/`. If the browser
blocks autoplay with sound, click the opening frame or press a key to start the
clip with its soundtrack. The server reads the
September 1994 retail files from `Qwebgpu/MS-DOS`, a symlink to the
repository's `MS-DOS` directory. On a fresh tab, `BQUAR1DK.AVI` plays
before the startup screens. Use the manual to enter the three-digit
response if copy protection is enabled. Press a key or tap the animated title to reach the main menu. Use Up/Down
or W/S to select and Enter or Space to activate. The mouse highlights a row
on hover and opens it on click. Load opens the five retail save
slots. Enter selects a slot, Y/N answers the restore prompt, and Escape leaves
the slot list. **New Game** loads the Core and fades from WAIT.IMG into the
cab. A key or click dismisses the retail welcome text. Escape opens the
menu and Resume returns to the cab. **Quit Game** plays `BQUAR2DK.AVI` and returns to the
logo and title sequence. Space, Escape, or a click on the video skips it.
Reloading the same tab retains the opening video's play state and a
successful manual response. If an AVI is absent from the game directory,
startup or restart continues without that clip.

## Configuration

The served [config.json](config.json) contains the default settings. Set
`"copyProtection": true` and reload the page to enable the original manual
challenge; it is disabled by default. When enabled, `COPY.IMG`, `COPYFONT.SPR`
and `PW.ENC` are required in the selected game folder. This setting does not
modify the DOS executable or its disassembly.

Set `"demo": true` to use the shareware files from `Qwebgpu/QDEMO`
(linked to `DEMO/`); `"demo": false` retains the retail SKIPIT path.
Demo mode plays `lakeshore.mp4` first while loading the Core assets in the
background. The red `QTITLE.GIF` screen shows the current filename and the
percentage of startup jobs completed before entering the
same native driving loop as SKIPIT using demo data. The demo uses WASD or the arrow keys to drive and J or Z for its power-60 jump. Press N to engage nitro. The demo starts with three charges. Each press sets the cab speed to 600 and requires 40 driving ticks before the next charge can fire.
Both modes use the same world-space asphalt on Core road tiles 0–2, with color sampled from the original unmarked pavement. Wear, aggregate and narrow cracks vary across cell boundaries. Chase view displays the live navigation CRT face-on at the lower right.
The demo starts at full display resolution. Minus and plus step between 320×200 and monitor resolution. P pauses until the
next key press. Escape opens the
Y/N exit prompt. Y plays the original credits for 180 retraces and N
resumes driving. The title and credits use the demo's nine-unit DAC fade.
The existing night cubemap uses the purple from SKIPIT Core palette entry
81 (six-bit DAC 8, 4, 10), independently of the optional HD skyline key.
The map uses the demo overlays' palette without the still-unrecovered palette
overrides. Demo Core loads the original mine, wall gun, muzzle flash, tracer and missile assets. Fixed hazards use QDEMO’s four-phase scanner and its own placement and sprite tables. Demo engine audio, other actors, player weapons, fares and the
original menu are not yet ported; demo shading and other renderer differences
also need work. This is not yet demo parity.
The demo jump and gravity updates use QDEMO's rate-adjusted movement table
(`floor(12*i/rate)`, minimum one for nonzero entries), evaluated at the browser's
fixed 35 Hz step; retail uses a different vertical-motion path.
The power-60 jump also seeds QDEMO's rate-adjusted landing counter; the browser
consumes that counter at touchdown and preserves its residual power. The queued
QDEMO landing sound remains part of the unported audio path.
The native path does not run an executable or use an emulator, DOS4GW or
retail game files. [Driving comparison](../disassembly/QDEMO/README.md#shared-driving-code).
The demo path ignores `developerMode` and `copyProtection`; those settings
continue to apply to retail. `demo` takes precedence over the earlier
`shareware` setting when both are present.

Set `"fileUpload": true` and reload to show a **Select game folder** link.
The link opens the optional folder picker. A selected folder replaces the
served files for that session. Choose **Use served files** to return. The
upload option is disabled by default.

The web server must follow the symlink for the chosen `MS-DOS` or `QDEMO` directory. Anyone
who can reach the server can request files from that directory, including
save files; bind it to a trusted interface or restrict access. With folder
selection enabled, the browser reads the selected files through its local
file API. The menus and intro use the original 320×200
framebuffer with 4:3 display correction. Gameplay fills the browser viewport.

The included Qwebgpu service blocks `MS-DOS`, `graphics` and all but a small
HD allowlist in demo mode. The allowlist contains the shared taxi, monitor,
license plate and night cubemap, plus optional PNG replacements named
`QTITLE`, `SKY`, `CAB`, `LDOOR35` and `KFLOOR`. Only supply replacements
you have permission to distribute; an allowed filename is not a rights check.
The taxi and monitor are CC BY 4.0, and the sky is CC0 (see their attribution
below). Review the plate and all other files and their rights separately
before publishing. A generic static server, including the example above,
does not enforce these restrictions. This is not a distribution license or a
complete public-deployment audit. The included service only accepts
private-network clients; restart it after server changes.

The OVH deployment at `https://www.lakeshoretechnical.com/qdemo/` uses the
separate loopback-only `qdemo.service` and an nginx reverse proxy. Its
`QDEMO` directory contains the demo files; `MS-DOS`, the retail graphics
export and the retail `KSKY.png` replacement are excluded. Only the
demo-specific HD replacements and shared taxi, monitor, plate and night
cubemap are served. Deployment is a snapshot, not an automatic sync from
this repository. Run `./deploy.sh` from the repository to deploy manually.
It reads the SSH username, password and IP from the first three lines of
`~/.ovhcloud`, requires a previously trusted SSH host key, and expects the
nginx route and enabled user service to be installed already. It forces demo
mode in the deployed configuration without changing the local config, copies
only allowlisted HD assets, restarts the service, and retains the previous
snapshot outside the public web root. The service files are in
`services/qdemo/`.

### HD IMG replacements

Place a PNG in `Qwebgpu/HD` using the exact stem of the original IMG.
For example, `HD/KSKY.png` replaces `KSKY.IMG` in the Core when you start
a New Game. Both `.png` and `.PNG` work. Reload the page after adding an
image. The renderer uses the original IMG when a replacement is unavailable.

Match the original framing. The skyline uses the top half of its image, and
cockpit and door PNGs use alpha for their windows. Screen overlays keep
their original positions. Sprite banks and the floor texture atlas continue
to use their retail assets.

The [graphics export](graphics/) contains the 43 retail IMG rasters and 90 individual floor tiles. The [IMG format notes](../README.md#img) describe dimensions, tile numbering and palettes.

### Night sky and clouds

The renderer recognizes a nearly uniform color across the top of an HD sky
PNG. It keys out pixels of that color, including small variations in the Core's
purple fill, to reveal a star cubemap and moving volumetric clouds. The
black buildings wrap around the horizon. Each viewing ray selects a bearing
and elevation in the silhouette, keeping each rooftop at a fixed angle as the
camera turns or pitches. Both the retail IMG and the HD PNG use this mapping. The
key color tints the stars, atmosphere, clouds, and ambient lighting on city
surfaces. Districts without a keyed PNG take their tint from the retail palette.

The same six 2048 × 2048 cubemap faces are shared by all districts. The local
WebP stores a grayscale reprojection of Greg Zaal and Jarod Guest's 8K
night panorama so the renderer can apply each district's tint. [Qwantani Night (Pure Sky)](https://polyhaven.com/a/qwantani_night_puresky), [CC0 license](https://polyhaven.com/license).

The cloud pass ray-marches a 3D density texture through a layer above the
city. A world-space wind moves the density, and clouds obscure the stars as
they pass in front of them. Shallow views use less cloud opacity, and the
cloud highlights remain visible overhead as the sky darkens.

### Driving and display controls

| Input | Behavior |
| --- | --- |
| W, S or Up, Down | Apply forward thrust or brake. Holding S through a stop engages reverse. Release both to coast. |
| A, D or Left, Right | Turn the wheel left or right. Steering acts on signed speed and centers on release. |
| Alt | Suppress steering input and let the wheel center, as in the original keyboard path. |
| Enter | Sound the retail cab horn once. |
| F1, F2, F3 | Look out of the left window, through the windshield, or out of the right window. F2 restores the default dashboard framing. Both side views are level. |
| Z | Trigger the original hover impulse to hop over obstacles. |
| Mouse | Click the driving view to capture the pointer. The browser uses raw relative movement when available, falling back to ordinary pointer lock. Click again after returning from settings or a lost capture. Look up to 100° left or right and 45° up or down in the cockpit. In chase view, move horizontally or vertically to orbit the cab. Continuing upward after the boom reaches the cab's bottom moves the camera closer and tilts it toward the sky; moving back reverses this without changing the saved zoom. The camera stays pointed in its world direction when the cab turns, then smoothly follows the cab after two seconds without mouse movement, only while the cab is moving. |
| F | Toggle between cockpit and chase. New Game starts in the cockpit. Entering chase requests mouse capture for unlimited horizontal orbit; returning to the cockpit centers and levels the view. |
| V | Toggle the two white headlights. New Game starts with them on. |
| Mouse wheel | Zoom continuously in chase view, including while the pointer is captured. Roll forward to move closer. The distance is remembered when switching views. |
| M | Open the city map. Click a colored bay or use the arrow keys to select one. M or Escape returns to driving. |
| C or ] | Advance CD music to the next track, wrapping to track 02. C matches the DOS next-track control. |
| [ | Select the previous CD track. |
| P | Pause or resume CD music. |
| Tilde / backquote | Open Settings from the main menu or while driving. Tilde, Escape or Close returns to the game. |
| Minus, plus | Step the actual render resolution. Both the main keyboard and keypad work. The selected dimensions appear briefly. |
| Escape | Open the options menu. Resume retains position, wheel angle, momentum, camera and resolution. |

The lowest mode is 320×200. Widescreen steps include 640×360, 1280×720,
1920×1080 and 2560×1440 when they fit the display. Gameplay defaults to the
display's native pixel dimensions, subject to the device's texture limit.
Open Settings with tilde to set horizontal FOV from 60° to 120° in one-degree
steps. The default is 109° to frame the windshield and dashboard monitor. The 64° setting uses the retail projection. The zoom wheel controls the
chase boom independently. It shortens when it crosses a wall or deck, but
ignores geometry entirely above the cab so overhead gantries do not pull it in.
At minimum zoom it sits just above the modeled cab bottom; at maximum zoom
its unobstructed boom reaches 600 world units.

Settings also offers xBR-style texture upscaling from 2× to 4×, FXAA and
texture contrast-adaptive sharpening. Each starts off and is saved separately.
Texture processing applies to world materials and the taxi's base-color maps,
including at native gameplay resolution. The scaler uses a single-pass 45°
corner rule, not the full xBRz algorithm; taxi upscaling applies when the
texture is magnified. FXAA applies to the gameplay render only. None of these
effects filter the title, menus, map, video or fallback Wayfinder canvas.
An upper-right counter shows submitted render frames per second, averaged over
one second. Static menus may show zero; it is not a GPU completion or display
refresh measurement. Gameplay targets 60 FPS, but the counter does not
guarantee that the GPU or display presents every submitted frame.

The accepted exterior size is restored: the supplied GLB measures approximately
181.26 long, 68.29 wide and 61.65 high, including its sign. The temporary
20-unit modeled-eye calibration made the car too small beside street signs and
has been removed. These are replacement-model proportions, not recovered DOS
exterior dimensions. The HD cockpit retains its seat alignment and proportions.

Both retail and demo now present architecture at a uniform 2.3× scale on X, Y
and Z, with the Z=20 street plane fixed. Wall materials retain their original
proportions and floor texture coordinates retain their original repeat counts.
MAP cell origins, walls, raised floors, roof caps and wall lights use the same
transform. Antenna and rooftop assemblies scale their sprite dimensions along
with the spacing between pieces; shifting only their bases had created gaps.
Street lamps, stop signs and vehicles keep their accepted dimensions, while
their map positions follow the enlarged district. The camera follows that
placement and chase obstruction uses the displayed geometry. BSP depth ordering
converts the eye position back to original BLK coordinates on every axis.

This is an HD presentation adjustment. The decoded BLK records and simulation
collision/support tables retain the DOS values. Missile and tracer positions,
hit checks, damage and timing retain their existing simulation rules; a cab's
attached impact blast follows the rendered cab when it is on a raised floor.
Chase orbits the fitted model's vertical center. Wall contact uses five
120-unit probes. Floor support uses the center and four 100-unit probes.
The two white beams originate at the front faces of the GLB headlamp lenses and
follow the cab heading in either view. They illuminate indexed roads, walls and
placed sprites. The lenses and their depth-tested halos are visible from the front.
Beam range is 850 world units. The lighting model has no headlight shadow map.
The rear lenses have a dim running glow and become solid red with a brighter
glow and road spill while the brake/reverse key is held.

The taxi hides its wheel and hub assemblies while preserving the original model
fit and collision bounds. A baby-blue hover glow follows the cab and lights the
ground below it. The model uses its 28 embedded images at their supplied
resolution, including the 2048-pixel maps. Materials use their base color, metallic, roughness
and normal inputs. Window glass blends over the interior and street. The renderer
generates mipmaps for distant views and uses anisotropic filtering.
Chrome reflects a 128-pixel world cubemap centered on the cab. Settings controls
its face-update rate from Off to 60 updates per second; the default is 20 to
reduce rendering work. Off uses sky reflections instead. All six faces refresh
when the setting is enabled or changed; later updates can lag during fast
movement. Reflections do not include the cab or clouds.

The model is *FREE 1950s American Taxi (based on Checker)* by Libau Media. The
port applies placement and uniform scale at load time. [Original model](https://sketchfab.com/3d-models/free-1950s-american-taxi-based-on-checker-d1b10defaed94a1094419e84ca9b0bf1) · [CC BY 4.0](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by/4.0/).

If `HD/cars/taxi/commodore_64__computer_full_pack.glb` is present, its monitor
alone is fitted to the passenger dashboard, facing the passenger with a
10-degree turn toward the driver.
Its housing is shortened to fit inside the cab. Its rectangular
screen displays the Wayfinder arrows and text using `WAYP2.SPR` and the
district palette, without the original circular background, and with
animated CRT-style scanlines and glow. The monitor is placed inside the cab,
and its screen is dark from behind. The monitor is from *Commodore 64 ||
Computer (Full Pack)* by dark_igorek. [Original model](https://sketchfab.com/3d-models/commodore-64-computer-full-pack-1f43612fa2d54041bbe2bdff8164c2cd) · [CC BY 4.0](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by/4.0/).

Driving sound uses the installed `ENG7.VOC` loop and `IMPACT.VOC` sample.
Engine pitch follows the SKIPIT playback step in both directions. The browser
reduces engine gain relative to impacts; the original mixer's gain behavior
remains unresolved.
The impact sample uses the original indexed-effect rate calculation. The
head-on wall response requests the impact cue at `0x26489`. Missing or
unreadable driving samples are reported on entering the cab. Other original
effects and speech are not yet implemented; gameplay audio is not 1:1.

The city map assembles the four `KEMOMAP*.ZZZ` images and stamps the district's
`BAYn.DAT` locations using `MAPICONS.SPR`. The cab marker follows the heading
table in SKIPIT. Arrow keys select the nearest eligible bay within the chosen
90° sector; clicking a visible colored icon also selects it. The upper 160
lines scroll in single-pixel steps. The fixed
instrument strip shows the selected bay type, district and relative distance
using the retail map font. The selection carries into the cockpit Wayfinder,
which uses the eight direction lamps, type labels and range digits from
`WAYP2.SPR`. With the CRT model, the instrument is on its screen in both
views; without it, the cockpit uses the separate Wayfinder overlay. Manual
map waypoints are supported, but passenger-driven and other original
destination modes are not yet simulated.

Gameplay targets **60 presentations per second** with one full-detail path.
Three workers prepare the map and lookup tables, world geometry and materials,
and cockpit art concurrently. The GPU clips, projects, textures and
depth-tests the world, draws repeated blocks as instances, applies the district
wall material's backface, transparency and collision flags, and draws the
taxi from `HD/cars/taxi/taxi.glb`. Installations without the GLB use the retail-art cabin and exterior box. The browser renders the full finite MAP rather than SKIPIT's 49-cell neighborhood, grouping shared geometry into frustum-culled instances. Distant surfaces retain the dark palette and haze, while illuminated surfaces, emitters and halos remain visible. A separate 35 Hz fixed step runs
the keyboard driving equations. DOS used a measured frame-rate divisor there.
The intro and wrapper videos retain their own media clocks.

### Driving fidelity and engine state

The original initializes its driving variables at `0x1AD00` and updates them at
`0x1AD98`. Its steering input and centering rates are respectively 1000 and
1536 per frame-rate divisor. These are the high words of overlapping 32-bit
loads at `0x1AF1E` and `0x1AFD4`; an earlier reading reversed them. It
also maintains rotational displacement and rate at DS:1340C and DS:13428,
with a separate skid flag and previous displacement. The browser now carries
those variables through the steering, damping and thrust calculations. It
does not yet reproduce all the game-state events that can affect them, and
its fixed 35 Hz step replaces the original measured divisor. Handling,
including impacts, terrain effects and hover landing interactions,
is therefore not established as 1:1.
Hover ascent samples reverse-facing overhead floors at the cab center using
the original 50-unit clearance. On touchdown,
the browser applies the retail landing displacement sequence to the driver’s eye. Other vertical effects and their event triggers remain incomplete.

The district actor scanner now activates mine-family MAP markers and the four
directional wall-gun blocks and five missile-launcher block types in KCITY. Mines use the retail XY and Z overlap,
district damage word, jump impulse, blast frames and pair of explosion cues.
Unarmoured cab health starts at 400 and is visible in the console. Directional
guns fire ETRACERS sprites on their sweep and burst schedule. Demo tracers apply
3 damage to an unarmoured cab; the retail tracer callback queues its hit reaction
without subtracting hull. The previous added 10-point retail penalty is removed.
Missile walls
alternate their two retail firing positions and headings, play FIREBELC.VOC,
and use directional EMISSILE sprites. A head-on missile impact follows the cab for its twelve blast frames. KCITY missiles deal 70 unarmoured damage. The browser uses
its existing wall sweep for projectile obstruction. Tracer centers stay at world
Z=-20 in demo and Z=-16 in retail, at DOS scale 192 (half the gun's 384).
Missiles launch at Z=-16 and add four per update while below zero. Both games'
logical hit checks accept absolute projectile Z >= -25 and strict XY offsets
inside ±48; those checks do not use the replacement model's bounds or the camera.
Tracers check cab contact before wall obstruction; missiles check obstruction
first. A projectile can therefore register a hit outside the visible mesh.
The original contact region is retained instead of bending shots toward the
replacement body. The complete damage, death,
shared actor scheduling and other weapon paths remain open.

The inaccessible backs of one-sided facades render opaque black below their
existing roofline, with depth occlusion and no lighting, haze or emission.
Street-facing artwork and authored two-sided walls retain their textures.
Missing roofs over inaccessible building volumes are closed with opaque black
caps inferred from the solid one-sided facades. Streets, courtyards, masked
openings and authored two-sided walls are excluded from the inferred boundaries.
The caps participate in depth occlusion but receive no textures, light, haze or
emission, and do not change collision or floor support. The hover light is
evaluated on the receiving floor.

SKIPIT loads per-district collision and trigger tables and initializes a
128-byte district state array with district-specific values at `0x45340`.
Its save routines serialize 104 address/size ranges, including an array of 50
124-byte records, and restore runtime references afterward. Qwebgpu instead
keeps browser objects for the current car, loaded district, waypoint and
session; it loads the collision/trigger tables but does not yet implement the
original district state machine or save payload restoration. Resume retains
the browser session, not a complete DOS game state.

The cab collects placed street fixtures in SKIPIT’s 49-cell draw order. Within
each block it uses the BSP and orders placements in each leaf by decreasing
distance. A moving cab can contact an origin less than 50 units to either
side and less than 100 units ahead, or behind when reversing. The first
qualifying placement supplies the page and image in `OBJ%d.DAT`.
Classes 4, 5 and 6 give a quarter-, full- or double-speed sideways push.
Class 7 stops the cab, 8 reverses part of its speed, and 9 changes the
speed accumulator. The next driving tick consumes the selected contact before
the wall sweep. The gameplay view stays with the cab during free look and chase.
If a distributed BSP diverges from the BLK directory, complete blocks keep
their decoded contact order. Each remaining block uses one distance-ordered
leaf. Equal-distance contacts use the DOS sort’s tie ordering. [Placement projection and response](street-contact.js).

Settings saves horizontal FOV, vertical mouse inversion, image processing and separate Sound FX and CD audio
volumes under `qwebgpu.settings` in localStorage for the current browser origin.
Both volumes start at 100%. Sound FX includes the
engine, impacts, menu sounds, voices and AVI audio. CD audio controls the
driving tracks and intro CD music. Changing the Sound FX slider plays the menu
click as a preview. Driving pauses while Settings is open.

Expand **Developer console** in driving Settings to see the current position, MAP word and block variant, camera,
render size and district draw counts. `map` prints nearby cells; `walls` lists
the current block's wall endpoints, material flags, texture availability and
facing. `walls dx dy` inspects a neighboring cell. `floors [dx dy]` reports
floor vertices, material, texture parameters and visible side. `report` prints the current
state, map, walls and floors together; `copy` copies that report and the console log
when clipboard access is available. `help` lists commands, and Escape closes
the console without opening the game menu.

To test from another computer, use HTTPS or forward the loopback server over
SSH. Run this command on the client with your Arch username and host address,
then open `http://127.0.0.1:8000/` on that computer.

```sh
ssh -N -L 8000:127.0.0.1:8000 user@arch-host
```

## Current startup sequence

| DOS entry | Browser implementation |
|---|---|
| `0x47C44` | When enabled in the config, reads the prompt strings and character widths from the player's executable, chooses a record in `PW.ENC`, and draws the three-attempt challenge using `COPY.IMG` and the planar glyphs in `COPYFONT.SPR`. |
| `0x2AFF4` | Shows `GAMETEK.IMG` and plays `EXPLO1.VOC`. |
| `0x2B084` | Shows `IEGLOW.IMG` and plays `HYPNO.VOC`. |
| `0x2B0FC` | Plays `TITLE.FLI` up to four times, starts `QTITLE.VOC` once, and polls for input. |
| `0x2AA7C`–`0x2ADFB` | Draws OPTION.IMG and the selected transparent OPTIONC.SPR overlay at coordinates read from the executable. Cold-start navigation skips Resume and Save. |

If browser autoplay blocks audio, the two splash screens continue silently
after the original 180-retrace no-sound delay instead of waiting for input.
Audio becomes available after a user gesture.
| `0x46FC0`, `0x471B4`, `0x4756C`, `0x4779C` | Opens LOADSCR.IMG, reads the five local save headers and draws their descriptions and prompts with SAVEFONT.SPR and ASKFONT.SPR. |
| `0x2C198` | Fades in WAIT.IMG on entry to the game-loading path. |
| `0x2C0F8`, `0x4289C`, `0x310DC` | Reads the district filenames and spawn from SKIPIT.EXE. New Game selects KCITY, loads the map, block geometry and district table pages, and starts at cell (135, 19). |
| `0x2BB24`, `0x2F828`, `0x3D2C0` | Composes the initial stationary street and cab view, draws the neutral wheel, and overlays the two-line welcome prompt before fading up with the KFLOOR palette. |

## The unattended intro and CD music

Let the title run for four passes. DOS follows it with the DRAKESS stills in
order 4, 2, 3, 6, then plays INTRO.FLI and draws the opening Kemo history over
the retained movie frame. The text comes from SKIPIT.EXE and the glyph masks
come from FONT.SPR. Input during the stills returns to the title. A key during
the title opens the menu.

The local FLI decoder handles COLOR_64, LC, BLACK, BRUN and COPY chunks.
The crawl brightens background pixels using four shade tables. The browser
uses an exhaustive nearest-color match. The DOS executable uses a bounded
palette-tree search, so some glyph pixels can differ.

CD audio is optional. For the split *Hard Rock Cab (Japan)* image in PS1/,
run this PowerShell line from inside the `PS1` folder with FFmpeg on your PATH.

```powershell
foreach ($track in 2..12) { $n = '{0:D2}' -f $track; ffmpeg -hide_banner -loglevel error -n -f s16le -ar 44100 -ch_layout stereo -i "Hard Rock Cab (Japan) (Track $n).bin" -c:a flac "../MS-DOS/track$n.flac" }
```

Track 01 holds PlayStation data. Each of tracks 02 through 12 is stereo
16-bit little-endian PCM at 44,100 Hz with INDEX 01 at 00:02:00 in its own
file. The command preserves every sample, including the pregap, and keeps
existing FLACs. The browser
uses its native FLAC decoder and starts track02.flac at two seconds during
INTRO.FLI. Audio continues through the crawl and stops on return to the title.
New Game starts driving music at track 03. Opening the options menu and
choosing Resume preserves the playhead and pause state. Selecting a track
skips its two-second pregap. The browser-specific previous-track and pause
controls supplement the DOS C key. Missing tracks or playback errors appear
briefly in the game and in both the game and browser developer consoles.

The street renderer uses row-major retail wall art, per-face floor scale and
rotation, and the placed sprites in the fourth BLK group. MAP IDs resolve through SKIPIT's descriptor table. Two cells east of spawn,
MAP ID 0x6E selects BLK record 0x6D, which has eight floors, sixteen wall runs
and ten sprite placements. GPU distance shading uses the district RGB target and the
executable's 64 depth bands. For coplanar surfaces within a block, the renderer reads the retail BSP
and uses the camera's position relative to each split plane to break depth ties.
Overlapping walls in adjacent cells still use the panel-area rule. Animated
materials use the active bank, image and masking flags from the district ANIM
sequences. Wall contact spans a complete BLK run. The cab follows supporting
floors using a center probe and four corner probes. Full airborne motion and
pixel-level parity remain open. [Spawn geometry trace](../README.md#the-structure-east-of-the-new-game-spawn).

The darker street uses the full district sky tint; chrome reflections use a more neutral, subdued version. Core streetlights cast the color sampled from each fixture’s artwork. Stop signs cast red from their electronic lettering. The yellow wall panels add a broad warm spill onto the road and cab, with softer highlights on the cab materials. Red wall and overhead bulbs also add a broader cab-only contribution, leaving the street lighting unchanged. Wall halos follow the side carrying the artwork and fade edge-on. The two Omnicorp sign panels cast a broader red light across the façade and pavement. [Sky palette trace and lighting parameters](../README.md#sky-color-and-street-lighting).

## AVI playback

A module worker reads the game AVI, parses the RIFF stream
lists and `movi` records, and decodes the retail 320×184 TrueMotion RGB555
keyframes. Playback reuses one transferable frame buffer. WebGPU expands
the packed RGB555 into RGB, and the Web Audio clock keeps the picture in
step with the original mono 22,050 Hz PCM. Movie frames can be decoded
independently, so the worker can catch up to the audio clock without
decoding intervening frames.

The two retail AVIs use compression mode 6, delta set 0, and vector table 1.
The decoder supports that profile. A few packets end on the final pixel
pair without the next predictor byte. Playback keeps the available delta
in those cases. The adapted decoder and codebook are distributed under
LGPL-2.1-or-later. [Decoder](vendor/truemotion1/decode.js), [license](vendor/truemotion1/COPYING.LGPLv2.1).

The original DOS launch order is recorded in the
[installer trace and AVI layout](../README.md#installer-generated-batch-files).

## Executing the recovered screen

The browser keeps four 16,000-byte VGA planes and a 256-entry DAC palette.
Its GPU shader fetches the index from the corresponding plane for each
pixel, then looks up the color on the GPU. Startup IMG files are decoded as
indices to preserve duplicate palette entries. Fades update six-bit DAC
components in two half-palette writes per pass.

The [JavaScript sprite blitter](indexed-screen.js) implements the opaque and
transparent DOS routines at `0x234BC` and `0x232CC`. The [startup routines](dos-assets.js)
use 32-bit multiplication for the Watcom random step at `0x537BC` and the
signed AX digit comparison at `0x47F14`.

The [renderer](display.js) calls WebGPU directly. FLI frames
upload from the decoder's linear index array. For game screens the same
shader addresses four VGA planes. Palette fades reuse the index texture.
The browser port runs as local JavaScript modules and requires no build step.

### Production package

Run `npm ci --no-audit --no-fund` and `npm run build` in `Qwebgpu` to generate
`dist/`. The production entry, separate district/AVI workers and stylesheet have
hashed filenames. JavaScript uses esbuild's ES2022 minification with source maps;
the build does not enable unsafe arithmetic transforms. Worker URLs point to the
generated worker filenames. Brotli sidecars accompany the text resources.
Game files and HD assets remain separate from this generated code package.

`../deploy.sh` builds this package and stages it with the existing allowlisted
assets. Both included servers negotiate precompressed `.br` resources and mark
hashed JavaScript/CSS immutable for one year. HTML and configuration remain
uncached. The LAN service continues serving the editable source modules.

`vendor/truemotion1/` is copied byte-for-byte, with its readable decoder,
codebook and `COPYING.LGPLv2.1`; it remains external to the minified bundles.
The renderer and GLB loader have no Three.js dependency. Model loading uses
only the local affine math operations in `model-math.js`.

Pavement uses a periodic 256×256 R8 noise tile with all nine mip levels, baked
once per district load, and explicit shader gradients. Its world-space noise
scales are retained, with an eight-cell repeat. Gameplay presentation interpolates
the fixed-step cab pose; paused screens use the current pose. The 35 Hz simulation
and phase-locked 60 Hz presentation schedule retain their original arithmetic.
World and taxi texture filters remain in their material shaders; optional FXAA
uses one presentation pass and is bypassed when disabled.

Taxi base-color maps already use hardware sRGB decoding. HD skyline, dashboard,
door and menu replacements still composite in the existing encoded color space;
they do not have the manual linearization described in the audit. Their raw
views are retained so the indexed palette, skyline key and fades keep matching.
The indexed-material sharpening path still needs its linearization helpers.

Display teardown clears its FPS interval and releases its GPU resources on game
exit or startup failure. Existing worker handoff termination, listener abort
controllers, halo depth-write settings and reflection draw/upload gates remain.
The optional dynamic-offset uniform ring and pure-data module consolidation were
not applied.

When the manual challenge is enabled, the player types into the game screen. Enter submits, Backspace moves left,
and another digit at the final position overwrites it. Touching the screen
requests the mobile keyboard. The prompt, advances, bitmap font, key-click
sample and result messages come from the selected installation.

Browser wall time supplies the random seed. The browser schedules redraws
and retraces, so cursor cadence, palette transitions and audio timing can vary
with the device. A DOS framebuffer comparison, complete impact and terrain
physics, and restoring the save payload remain pending. The browser reads the selected save header and leaves the
original file untouched.


────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

.\roof-caps.js
────────────────────────

// Fill the building side of opaque one-sided facades inside a MAP cell.
// A shear makes every integer-coordinate wall non-horizontal for the sweep,
// including a lone facade whose building continues into the neighboring cell.
// The resulting trapezoids need no polygon fan across streets or courtyards.
const shear=Math.SQRT2, epsilon=0.000001;

function clipWall(a,b,cellSize) {
  const dx=b[0]-a[0], dy=b[1]-a[1];
  let first=0, last=1;
  for (const [p,q] of [[-dx,a[0]],[dx,cellSize-a[0]],[-dy,a[1]],[dy,cellSize-a[1]]]) {
    if (p===0) { if (q<0) return null; continue; }
    const t=q/p;
    if (p<0) first=Math.max(first,t);
    else last=Math.min(last,t);
    if (first>=last) return null;
  }
  return [[a[0]+first*dx,a[1]+first*dy],[a[0]+last*dx,a[1]+last*dy]];
}

export function roofCaps(block,flagsFor,cellSize=512) {
  const edges=new Map();
  for (const face of block.faces) {
    if (face.floor || face.vertices.length<4 || face.material>=block.variantStride ||
        (block.surfaceNodes?.[face.surface] ?? 0)<0) continue;
    // Masked, two-sided and nonblocking walls are not building boundaries.
    if (flagsFor(face.sideMaterial)&11) continue;
    const clipped=clipWall(face.vertices[0],face.vertices[1],cellSize);
    if (!clipped) continue;
    const [a,b]=clipped;
    const key=p => p.map(v => Math.round(v/epsilon)).join(',');
    const forward=key(a)+'/'+key(b), backward=key(b)+'/'+key(a);
    const top=Math.min(...face.vertices.map(p => p[2]));
    if (edges.has(forward)) edges.get(forward).top=Math.min(edges.get(forward).top,top);
    else edges.set(forward,{a,b,top,backward});
  }
  // Oppositely authored faces describe a divider with two visible sides.
  const walls=[...edges.values()].filter(edge => !edges.has(edge.backward)).map(({a,b,top}) => {
    const av=a[1]+shear*a[0], bv=b[1]+shear*b[0];
    return {a:a[0],v:av,slope:(b[0]-a[0])/(bv-av),
      low:Math.min(av,bv),high:Math.max(av,bv),direction:Math.sign(bv-av),top};
  });
  if (!walls.length) return [];
  const at=(wall,v) => wall.a+(v-wall.v)*wall.slope;
  const bands=[0,cellSize,shear*cellSize,(1+shear)*cellSize,...walls.flatMap(wall => [wall.low,wall.high])];
  for (let i=0; i<walls.length; i++) for (let j=i+1; j<walls.length; j++) {
    const a=walls[i], b=walls[j], slope=a.slope-b.slope;
    if (Math.abs(slope)<epsilon) continue;
    const v=(b.a-b.v*b.slope-a.a+a.v*a.slope)/slope;
    if (v>Math.max(a.low,b.low)+epsilon && v<Math.min(a.high,b.high)-epsilon) bands.push(v);
  }
  bands.sort((a,b) => a-b);
  const left=v => Math.max(0,(v-cellSize)/shear);
  const right=v => Math.min(cellSize,v/shear);
  const caps=[];
  for (let i=1; i<bands.length; i++) {
    const low=bands[i-1], high=bands[i];
    if (high-low<epsilon) continue;
    const middle=(low+high)/2;
    const active=walls.filter(wall => wall.low<middle && wall.high>middle)
      .sort((a,b) => at(a,middle)-at(b,middle));
    // A building can continue through a cell corner beyond both endpoints
    // of its facade. That band is a single region: inherit the side of the
    // nearest real boundary instead of leaving an uncovered corner triangle.
    if (!active.length) {
      const x=(left(middle)+right(middle))/2, y=middle-shear*x;
      let nearest, distance=Infinity, inside=false;
      for (const wall of walls) {
        const ax=at(wall,wall.low), ay=wall.low-shear*ax;
        const bx=at(wall,wall.high), by=wall.high-shear*bx;
        const dx=bx-ax, dy=by-ay;
        const t=Math.max(0,Math.min(1,((x-ax)*dx+(y-ay)*dy)/(dx*dx+dy*dy)));
        const d=(x-ax-t*dx)**2+(y-ay-t*dy)**2;
        if (d<distance) {
          distance=d; nearest=wall;
          inside=wall.direction*(dx*(y-ay)-dy*(x-ax))>epsilon;
        }
      }
      if (inside) {
        const point=(u,v) => [u,v-shear*u,nearest.top];
        caps.push([point(left(low),low),point(right(low),low),
          point(right(high),high),point(left(high),high)]);
      }
      continue;
    }
    const boundaries=[{position:left,direction:0,top:null},
      ...active.map(wall => ({position:v => at(wall,v),direction:wall.direction,top:wall.top})),
      {position:right,direction:0,top:null}];
    for (let j=1; j<boundaries.length; j++) {
      const a=boundaries[j-1], b=boundaries[j];
      if (b.position(middle)-a.position(middle)<epsilon || !(a.direction<0 || b.direction>0)) continue;
      // Recess the cap to the lower adjacent roofline when heights differ;
      // never raise a black slab above a shorter facade.
      const z=a.top===null ? b.top : b.top===null ? a.top : Math.max(a.top,b.top);
      const point=(edge,v) => {
        const x=Math.max(left(v),Math.min(right(v),edge.position(v)));
        return [x,v-shear*x,z];
      };
      caps.push([point(a,low),point(b,low),point(b,high),point(a,high)]);
    }
  }
  return caps;
}

const storageKey = 'qwebgpu.settings';
let saved;
try { saved = JSON.parse(localStorage.getItem(storageKey)); } catch {}
const volume = n => Number.isFinite(n) ? Math.min(100, Math.max(0, Math.round(n))) : 100;
const strength = n => Number.isFinite(n) ? Math.min(100,Math.max(0,Math.round(n))) : 0;
export const settings = {
  fov: Number.isFinite(saved?.fov) ? Math.max(60,Math.min(120,Math.round(saved.fov))) : 109,
  invertY: saved?.invertY === true,
  effects: volume(saved?.effects),
  cd: volume(saved?.cd),
  upscale: Number.isFinite(saved?.upscale) ? Math.min(4,Math.max(1,Math.round(saved.upscale))) : 1,
  fxaa: strength(saved?.fxaa),
  sharpen: strength(saved?.sharpen),
  reflections: Number.isFinite(saved?.reflections) ? Math.min(60,Math.max(0,Math.round(saved.reflections/5)*5)) : 20,
};

export const settingsPanel = document.querySelector('#settingsPanel');
const invert = document.querySelector('#invertY');
const cdPlayers = new Set();
let effectsBus;

export function effectsOutput(context) {
  if (!effectsBus || effectsBus.context !== context) {
    effectsBus = context.createGain();
    effectsBus.gain.value = settings.effects / 100;
    effectsBus.connect(context.destination);
  }
  return effectsBus;
}

export function bindCdVolume(player) {
  player.volume = settings.cd / 100;
  cdPlayers.add(player);
  return () => cdPlayers.delete(player);
}

function save() {
  try { localStorage.setItem(storageKey, JSON.stringify(settings)); } catch {}
}

invert.checked = settings.invertY;
invert.addEventListener('change', () => {
  settings.invertY = invert.checked;
  save();
});
for (const [name,id,unit] of [['effects','effectsVolume','%'],['cd','cdVolume','%'],['fov','fov','°'],
  ['upscale','upscale','×'],['fxaa','fxaa','%'],['sharpen','sharpen','%'],['reflections','reflections','/s']]) {
  const slider = document.getElementById(id);
  const output = document.querySelector('output[for="' + id + '"]');
  const paint = () => {
    const off = name === 'upscale' ? slider.valueAsNumber === 1 :
      (name === 'fxaa' || name === 'sharpen' || name === 'reflections') && slider.valueAsNumber === 0;
    output.value = off ? 'Off' : slider.value + unit;
    slider.style.setProperty('--level', (slider.valueAsNumber-slider.min)*100/(slider.max-slider.min)+'%');
    slider.setAttribute('aria-valuetext', output.value);
  };
  slider.value = settings[name];
  paint();
  slider.addEventListener('input', () => {
    settings[name] = slider.valueAsNumber;
    paint();
    if (name === 'cd') {
      for (const player of cdPlayers) player.volume = settings.cd / 100;
    } else if (name === 'effects' && effectsBus) {
      effectsBus.gain.setTargetAtTime(settings.effects / 100, effectsBus.context.currentTime, 0.012);
    }
    save();
  });
}

settingsPanel.addEventListener('keydown', event => {
  event.stopPropagation();
  if (event.code === 'Backquote' || event.key === 'Escape') {
    event.preventDefault();
    if (!event.repeat) settingsPanel.close();
  }
});

export function openSettings(previewEffect) {
  const events = new AbortController();
  const slider = document.querySelector('#effectsVolume');
  slider.addEventListener('change', previewEffect, {signal:events.signal});
  return new Promise(resolve => {
    settingsPanel.addEventListener('close', () => {
      events.abort();
      resolve();
    }, {once:true});
    settingsPanel.showModal();
    invert.focus({preventScroll:true});
  });
}

import {uploadBitmap} from './hd-images.js';

let stars;
const resources = new WeakMap();

export function loadStarCubemap() {
  stars ??= fetch(new URL('./HD/night-cubemap.webp', import.meta.url)).then(async response => {
    if (!response.ok) throw new Error(`Night cubemap returned HTTP ${response.status}.`);
    return createImageBitmap(await response.blob());
  }).catch(error => {
    console.warn('Unable to load the night cubemap.', error);
    return null;
  });
  return stars;
}

// A keyed panorama has a nearly uniform, opaque strip above the silhouettes.
export function skylineKey(bitmap) {
  if (!bitmap) return null;
  const sample = document.createElement('canvas');
  sample.width = 96;
  sample.height = 12;
  const context = sample.getContext('2d');
  if (!context) return null;
  context.imageSmoothingEnabled = false;
  context.drawImage(bitmap, 0, 0, bitmap.width, Math.max(1, Math.floor(bitmap.height / 8)),
    0, 0, sample.width, sample.height);
  const {data} = context.getImageData(0, 0, sample.width, sample.height);
  const pixels = [];
  for (let i = 0; i < data.length; i += 4) {
    if (data[i + 3] === 255) pixels.push([data[i], data[i + 1], data[i + 2]]);
  }
  if (pixels.length < 0.85 * data.length / 4) return null;
  const key = [0, 1, 2].map(channel => {
    const values = pixels.map(pixel => pixel[channel]).sort((a, b) => a - b);
    return values[values.length >> 1];
  });
  const matching = pixels.filter(pixel => pixel.every((value, channel) => Math.abs(value - key[channel]) <= 12));
  return matching.length >= 0.85 * pixels.length ? key.map(value => value / 255) : null;
}

const noiseSize = 64;

// Periodic value noise, with four scales stored in one small 3D texture.
function buildCloudNoise() {
  let seed = 0x6f2ac17b;
  function random() {
    seed ^= seed << 13;
    seed ^= seed >>> 17;
    seed ^= seed << 5;
    return (seed >>> 0) / 0x100000000;
  }
  const interpolate = (a, b, t) => a + (b - a) * t;
  const noise = new Float32Array(noiseSize ** 3);
  for (const [frequency, weight] of [[4, 0.52], [8, 0.25], [16, 0.15], [32, 0.08]]) {
    const lattice = Float32Array.from({length: frequency ** 3}, random);
    const axes = Array.from({length: noiseSize}, (_, i) => {
      const coordinate = i * frequency / noiseSize;
      const index = Math.floor(coordinate);
      const frac = coordinate - index;
      return [index, (index + 1) % frequency, frac * frac * (3 - 2 * frac)];
    });
    let index = 0;
    for (const [z0, z1, zt] of axes) {
      for (const [y0, y1, yt] of axes) {
        const a = (z0 * frequency + y0) * frequency;
        const b = (z0 * frequency + y1) * frequency;
        const c = (z1 * frequency + y0) * frequency;
        const d = (z1 * frequency + y1) * frequency;
        for (const [x0, x1, xt] of axes) {
          const lower = interpolate(interpolate(lattice[a + x0], lattice[a + x1], xt),
            interpolate(lattice[b + x0], lattice[b + x1], xt), yt);
          const upper = interpolate(interpolate(lattice[c + x0], lattice[c + x1], xt),
            interpolate(lattice[d + x0], lattice[d + x1], xt), yt);
          noise[index++] += weight * interpolate(lower, upper, zt);
        }
      }
    }
  }
  return Uint8Array.from(noise, value => Math.round(value * 255));
}

export function skyResources(device, bitmap) {
  if (resources.has(device)) return resources.get(device);
  const validFaceSize = bitmap && bitmap.width / 3 === bitmap.height / 2 && bitmap.width % 3 === 0;
  const size = validFaceSize ? bitmap.width / 3 : 1;
  const cube = device.createTexture({size: [size, size, 6], format: 'rgba8unorm',
    usage: GPUTextureUsage.TEXTURE_BINDING | GPUTextureUsage.COPY_DST | GPUTextureUsage.RENDER_ATTACHMENT});
  if (validFaceSize) {
    // Atlas cells are in WebGPU face order: +X, -X, +Y, -Y, +Z, -Z.
    for (let face = 0; face < 6; face++) {
      const x = size * (face % 3), y = size * Math.floor(face / 3);
      uploadBitmap(device,bitmap,{texture:cube,origin:[0,0,face]},size,size,x,y);
    }
  }
  const noise = device.createTexture({size: [noiseSize, noiseSize, noiseSize], dimension: '3d', format: 'r8unorm',
    usage: GPUTextureUsage.TEXTURE_BINDING | GPUTextureUsage.COPY_DST});
  device.queue.writeTexture({texture: noise}, buildCloudNoise(),
    {bytesPerRow: noiseSize, rowsPerImage: noiseSize}, [noiseSize, noiseSize, noiseSize]);
  const value = {
    cube: cube.createView({dimension: 'cube'}),
    imageSampler: device.createSampler({magFilter: 'linear', minFilter: 'linear'}),
    density: noise.createView(),
    densitySampler: device.createSampler({magFilter: 'linear', minFilter: 'linear',
      addressModeU: 'repeat', addressModeV: 'repeat', addressModeW: 'repeat'}),
  };
  resources.set(device, value);
  return value;
}

export const cloudTargetFormat = 'rgba8unorm';
export const cloudResolutionDivisor = 4;

export function prepareCloudPipeline(device, sharedShader) {
  const module = device.createShaderModule({code: sharedShader + `
@group(0) @binding(0) var<uniform> view: View;
@group(0) @binding(1) var densitySampler: sampler;
@group(0) @binding(2) var densityVolume: texture_3d<f32>;

const cloudBase = 1100.0;
const cloudTop = 2300.0;

fn density(position: vec3f, wind: vec2f) -> f32 {
  let altitude = (position.y - cloudBase) / (cloudTop - cloudBase);
  let profile = smoothstep(0.0, 0.20, altitude) * (1.0 - smoothstep(0.62, 1.0, altitude));
  let moved = position - vec3f(wind.x, 0.0, wind.y);
  let shape = textureSampleLevel(densityVolume, densitySampler, moved * vec3f(0.00015, 0.00062, 0.00015), 0.0).r;
  let detail = textureSampleLevel(densityVolume, densitySampler, moved * 0.00072 + vec3f(0.37, 0.14, 0.61), 0.0).r;
  return smoothstep(0.49, 0.72, shape * 0.83 + detail * 0.17) * profile;
}

@fragment fn clouds(@builtin(position) pos: vec4f) -> @location(0) vec4f {
  let size = max(vec2f(1.0), ceil(view.outputSize.xy / ${cloudResolutionDivisor}.0));
  let ray = normalize(skyRay(pos.xy / size));
  if (ray.y <= 0.04) { return vec4f(0.0); }
  let eye = vec3f(view.eye.x, -view.eye.z, view.eye.y);
  let first = max(0.0, (cloudBase - eye.y) / ray.y);
  let last = min(27000.0, (cloudTop - eye.y) / ray.y);
  if (last <= first) { return vec4f(0.0); }
  let step = (last - first) / 24.0;
  // Compress the optical path at shallow angles so scattered clouds do not merge into a solid band.
  let opticalStep = min(step, 2800.0 / 24.0);
  let wind = view.skyTint.w * vec2f(37.0, -19.0);
  var transmittance = 1.0;
  var light = 0.0;
  for (var i = 0; i < 24; i++) {
    let position = eye + ray * (first + (f32(i) + 0.5) * step);
    let amount = density(position, wind);
    if (amount > 0.002) {
      let shadow = density(position + vec3f(-170.0, 270.0, 160.0), wind);
      let scatter = mix(0.28, 0.87, exp(-3.4 * shadow));
      let opacity = 1.0 - exp(-amount * opticalStep * 0.0034);
      light += transmittance * opacity * scatter;
      transmittance *= 1.0 - opacity;
      if (transmittance < 0.018) { break; }
    }
  }
  let horizon = smoothstep(0.045, 0.10, ray.y);
  let opacity = (1.0 - transmittance) * horizon;
  let maximum = mix(0.76, 1.0, smoothstep(0.10, 0.55, ray.y));
  let soften = min(1.0, maximum / max(opacity, 0.001));
  return vec4f(vec3f(light * horizon * soften), opacity * soften);
}`});
  return device.createRenderPipelineAsync({layout: 'auto', vertex: {module, entryPoint: 'screen'},
    fragment: {module, entryPoint: 'clouds', targets: [{format: cloudTargetFormat}]},
    primitive: {topology: 'triangle-list'}});
}

.\street-contact.js
─────────────────────────────

// On a distance tie, reproduce the retail library sort (0x54D1D). Its
// three-slot pass and equal-pivot swaps can reorder equally distant objects.
function breakDistanceTie(objects,eyeX,eyeY,z,eligible) {
  const items = objects.map(obj => ({obj,d:(obj.x-eyeX)**2+(obj.y-eyeY)**2+(obj.z-1-z)**2}));
  function swap(i,j) { [items[i],items[j]] = [items[j],items[i]]; }
  function median(a,b,c) {
    return Math.max(Math.min(a,b),Math.min(Math.max(a,b),c));
  }
  function sort(start,end) {
    const count = end-start;
    if (count < 16) {
      for (const gap of [3,1]) {
        for (let i=start+gap; i<end; i+=gap) {
          for (let j=i; j>=start+gap && items[j].d > items[j-gap].d; j-=gap) swap(j,j-gap);
        }
      }
      return;
    }
    const mid = start+(count>>1), last = end-1;
    let pivot = items[mid].d;
    if (count > 29) {
      let outerLeft = items[start].d, outerRight = items[last].d;
      if (count > 42) {
        const k = count>>3;
        outerLeft = median(outerLeft,items[start+k].d,items[start+2*k].d);
        pivot = median(items[mid-k].d,pivot,items[mid+k].d);
        outerRight = median(items[last-2*k].d,items[last-k].d,outerRight);
      }
      pivot = median(outerLeft,pivot,outerRight);
    }
    let left = start, right = last, equalLeft = start, equalRight = last;
    while (left <= right) {
      while (left <= right && items[left].d >= pivot) {
        if (items[left].d === pivot) swap(left,equalLeft++);
        left++;
      }
      while (left <= right && items[right].d <= pivot) {
        if (items[right].d === pivot) swap(right,equalRight--);
        right--;
      }
      if (left <= right) swap(left++,right--);
    }
    // Move the equal groups from the ends into the middle of the partition.
    const leftSize = left-equalLeft, rightSize = equalRight-right;
    const l = Math.min(equalLeft-start,leftSize), r = Math.min(last-equalRight,rightSize);
    for (let i=0; i<l; i++) swap(start+i,left-l+i);
    for (let i=0; i<r; i++) swap(left+i,end-r+i);
    if (leftSize > 1) sort(start,start+leftSize);
    if (rightSize > 1) sort(end-rightSize,end);
  }
  sort(0,items.length);
  return items.find(({obj}) => eligible.has(obj)).obj;
}

// 0x40964 collects kind-zero placements in the district queue's drawing order.
// Keep the gameplay projection at the cab even while an exterior camera orbits.
export function projectedStreetContact(car,district,sin,cos) {
  const direction = Math.sign(car.velocity ?? (car.speed >> 8));
  if (!direction || !district.objectClasses) return null;
  const x = Math.trunc(car.x), y = Math.trunc(car.y), z = Math.trunc(car.z+car.seatOffset);
  const localX = x&511, localY = y&511;
  const originSide = -((-localX*cos+localY*sin) >> 11);
  const originDepth = (-localX*sin-localY*cos) >> 11;
  let queued = 0;
  for (const [dx,dy] of district.start.viewCells) {
    if (queued >= 600) break;
    const col = (x>>9)+dx, row = (y>>9)+dy;
    if (col < 0 || row < 0 || col >= district.width || row >= district.height) continue;
    const id = district.tiles[row*district.width+col]&127;
    if (!id) continue;
    const block = district.diagnostics.blocks[id];
    const eyeX = localX-512*dx, eyeY = localY-512*dy;
    const cx = 256-eyeX, cy = 256-eyeY;
    const side = -((cx*cos-cy*sin) >> 11);
    const far = ((cx*sin+cy*cos) >> 11)+257;
    if (far <= 0 || Math.max(0,Math.abs(side)-256)*256 >= 160*far) continue;
    queued += block.queueEntries ?? 0;
    if (!block.contactTree) continue;
    // The sprite projector adds separately rounded block and 32-unit local lookups.
    const cellSide = originSide+dx*((-cos*16)>>6)+dy*((sin*16)>>6);
    const cellDepth = originDepth+dx*((sin*16)>>6)+dy*((cos*16)>>6);
    function firstContact(tree) {
      if (!Array.isArray(tree)) {
        const first = tree.nx*eyeX+tree.ny*eyeY+tree.nz*z < tree.plane;
        return firstContact(first ? tree.first : tree.second) ??
          firstContact(first ? tree.second : tree.first);
      }
      // 0x3FF70 sorts a terminal list by decreasing squared distance.
      let best = null, farthest = -1, tied = null;
      for (const obj of tree) {
        const ix = obj.x>>5, iy = obj.y>>5;
        const lateral = cellSide+((-cos*ix)>>6)+((sin*iy)>>6);
        if (Math.abs(lateral) >= 50) continue;
        const ahead = direction*(cellDepth+((sin*ix)>>6)+((cos*iy)>>6));
        if (ahead <= 0 || ahead >= 100) continue;
        const distance = (obj.x-eyeX)**2+(obj.y-eyeY)**2+(obj.z-1-z)**2;
        if (distance < farthest) continue;
        const contact = {side:lateral,kind:district.objectClasses[obj.bank]?.[obj.classImage] ?? 0};
        if (distance === farthest) {
          tied ??= new Map([[best.obj,best.contact]]);
          tied.set(obj,contact);
        } else {
          farthest = distance;
          best = {obj,contact};
          tied = null;
        }
      }
      return tied ? tied.get(breakDistanceTie(tree,eyeX,eyeY,z,tied)) : best?.contact ?? null;
    }
    const contact = firstContact(block.contactTree);
    if (contact) return contact;
  }
  return null;
}

// 0x25E30 runs after the forward increment is calculated and before the wall sweep.
export function resolveStreetContact(car,step,sin,cos,hz) {
  const contact = car.streetContact;
  if (!contact) return;
  const speed = step.velocity;
  switch (contact.kind) {
    case 4:
    case 5:
    case 6: {
      const slide = contact.kind === 4 ? speed>>2 : contact.kind === 5 ? speed : speed*2;
      const negate = slide > 0 ? contact.side < 0 : contact.side > 0;
      const signedSlide = negate ? -slide : slide;
      step.dx += (Math.trunc(cos*signedSlide/hz)>>7)/8;
      step.dy += (Math.trunc(-sin*signedSlide/hz)>>7)/8;
      break;
    }
    case 7:
      step.velocity = 0;
      car.speed = 0;
      break;
    case 8:
      step.velocity = (speed>>1)-speed;
      car.speed = step.velocity*256;
      break;
    case 9:
      if (car.speed > 0xa00) car.speed = Math.max(0x3c00,car.speed-50*Math.max(1,Math.trunc(2400/hz)));
      break;
  }
}


────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

.\street-lights.js
────────────────────────────

// Two lighting bins along each 512-unit MAP tile. The GPU reads a directory
// of offsets followed by lamp IDs, with one shared record per placed emitter.
import {architectureScale,architectureCellSize} from './architecture.js';

export const lightBinSize = 256*architectureScale;

export function streetLightGrid({tiles,width,height,mesh,palette}) {
  const cols = width*2, rows = height*2;
  const counts = new Uint32Array(cols*rows);
  let total = 0;
  tiles.forEach((word,i) => {
    if (word&127) total += mesh.blockLights[(word&127)*4+((word>>>8)&3)].length;
  });
  const lights = new Float32Array(12*Math.max(1,total));
  let id = 0;
  tiles.forEach((word,i) => {
    if (!(word&127)) return;
    for (const [bx,by,z,radius,color,,intensity=1,rough=0,nx=0,ny=0,offset=0] of mesh.blockLights[(word&127)*4+((word>>>8)&3)]) {
      const c = color*3;
      lights.set([(i%width)*architectureCellSize+bx,Math.floor(i/width)*architectureCellSize+by,z,1/(radius*radius),
        intensity*(palette[c]/63)**2,intensity*(palette[c+1]/63)**2,
        intensity*(palette[c+2]/63)**2,rough,nx,ny,offset,0],12*id++);
    }
  });

  function visitBins(write) {
    for (let lamp=0; lamp<total; lamp++) {
      const at = lamp*12, x = lights[at], y = lights[at+1];
      // Round outward for the GPU's f32 distance and plane arithmetic.
      const radius = 1/Math.sqrt(lights[at+3])+0.1;
      const nx = lights[at+8], ny = lights[at+9], offset = lights[at+10];
      const left = Math.max(0,Math.floor((x-radius)/lightBinSize));
      const right = Math.min(cols-1,Math.floor((x+radius)/lightBinSize));
      const top = Math.max(0,Math.floor((y-radius)/lightBinSize));
      const bottom = Math.min(rows-1,Math.floor((y+radius)/lightBinSize));
      for (let row=top; row<=bottom; row++) {
        const dy = row*lightBinSize-y;
        const nearY = Math.max(0,dy,-dy-lightBinSize);
        for (let col=left; col<=right; col++) {
          const dx = col*lightBinSize-x;
          const nearX = Math.max(0,dx,-dx-lightBinSize);
          if (nearX*nearX+nearY*nearY > radius*radius) continue;
          const maxPlane = nx*dx+ny*dy+lightBinSize*(Math.max(0,nx)+Math.max(0,ny));
          if (maxPlane+offset < -0.15) continue;
          write(row*cols+col,lamp);
        }
      }
    }
  }
  visitBins(cell => counts[cell]++);
  let end = counts.length+1;
  for (let i=0; i<counts.length; i++) end += counts[i];
  const offsets = new Uint32Array(end);
  offsets[0] = counts.length+1;
  for (let i=0; i<counts.length; i++) {
    offsets[i+1] = offsets[i]+counts[i];
    counts[i] = offsets[i];
  }
  visitBins((cell,lamp) => { offsets[counts[cell]++] = lamp; });
  return {lights,offsets};
}

.\taxi-material.js
